Six readings are tempting and false:
\begin{itemize}
\item Source~101-90's $\kappa_r$ is \emph{not} the $\kappa_r$ of this report: it
is the OEIS-normalized $c(r)$ of \eqref{fce:eq:constant}, and its $\gamma_r$ is
this report's $\kappa_r$. Copied verbatim, its display
``$\kappa_r=\gamma_r/(\sqrt r\,2^{(r-1)/2})$'' would read
$\kappa_r=\kappa_r/(\sqrt r\,2^{(r-1)/2})$.
\item Source~101-90's $g$ (the $\gcd$ of the integers $h+s$) is $1$ for odd
$r$ and $2$ for even $r$: it is the time period $\nu$, with the
\emph{opposite} parity of $g_r=\gcd(2,r-1)$ of \eqref{fce:eq:steps}, which is
$2$ for odd $r$; indeed $g_r=2/\nu$.
\item Both reports use $z$ for the height variable and $u$ for marks; here
$u$ is the height and $z$ (with $y=\log z$) the marks, as in
Section~\ref{fce:sec:saddle}.
\item Source~101-86's $\beta$ is $\varphi^{-2}=(3-\sqrt5)/2$, not the exponent
$\beta=(r+1)/2$ of Section~\ref{fce:sec:inverse}, which source~101-90 calls
$\alpha_r$; neither is a coefficient $\alpha_{r,j}$.
\item The Gaussian-rule coefficients $\mathsf b_{r,j}$, $\mathsf b^\circ_j$
(the sources' $b_{r,j}$, $b_j$) are corrections in powers of the length $N$,
not the logarithmic coefficients $b_j$ of Section~\ref{fce:sec:inverse};
source~101-86's $\ell_2$ is that $b_2$.
\item The sources' $\nu_0,\nu$ are the inverse variable ($x_0,x$ here), not
the time period $\nu$; and $H_{\rm ex}$, $H_\circ$ are Hessians in
coordinates different from those of $H$ in \eqref{fce:eq:H}, with different
determinants (Proposition~\ref{fce:dx:prop:det}).
\end{itemize}

\section{Every fixed alphabet with the extreme steps unmarked (source~101-90)}\label{fce:dx:sec:alphabet}
\subsection{Zero steps for odd alphabets}\label{fce:dx:sec:zero}
For odd $r$ the step set \eqref{fce:eq:steps} contains $0$. Write
$\E_r(t)=\E(t,\ldots,t)$ for the length series of nonnegative excursions with
unit step weights, and $\E^\circ_r(t)$ for the same series without the zero
step; for a composition count, $A^\circ_r(n)$ counts zero-free nonnegative
excursions with $n$ copies of each nonzero step.

\begin{proposition}[Zero-step insertion (source~101-90)]\label{fce:dx:prop:zero}
For odd $r\ge3$,
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:zero}
 \E_r(t)=\frac1{1-t}\,\E^\circ_r\!\left(\frac t{1-t}\right),\qquad
 \kappa_r=\frac r{r-1}\,\E^\circ_r\!\left(\frac1{r-1}\right),
\end{equation}
and \textup{[write]}, at balanced content,
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:zerobal}
 A_r(n)=\binom{rn}{n}A^\circ_r(n)\qquad(n\ge0).
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof \textup{[write; source~101-90 states the identity and the insertion factor]}]
A zero step changes no height. Deleting the zero steps of a nonnegative
excursion of length $m+k$ with $k$ zero steps therefore leaves a zero-free
nonnegative excursion of length $m$, and conversely each of the
$\binom{m+k}k$ ways of inserting $k$ zero steps into a zero-free excursion
of length $m$ gives a nonnegative excursion; the two maps are inverse.
Hence the length-$N$ coefficient of $\E_r$ is
$\sum_{m+k=N}\binom{m+k}k[t^m]\E^\circ_r$, and
$\sum_k\binom{m+k}kt^k=(1-t)^{-m-1}$ gives the first identity. Both series
have nonnegative coefficients, and $\kappa_r=\E_r(1/r)$ is finite by
\eqref{fce:eq:criticalvalue}; letting $t\uparrow1/r$, monotone convergence
on both sides gives the second identity, with $t/(1-t)=1/(r-1)$ and
$1/(1-t)=r/(r-1)$ at $t=1/r$. At balanced content the deletion is a bijection
between the words counted by $A_r(n)$ and the pairs formed by a zero-free
word counted by $A^\circ_r(n)$ and a set of $n$ positions, among $rn$, for
the zero steps; this is \eqref{fce:dx:eq:zerobal}.
\end{proof}
The cases $r=3$ and $r=5$ of \eqref{fce:dx:eq:zerobal} are the derivation of
$A_3(n)$ in Section~\ref{fce:sec:constants} and
\eqref{fce:kk:eq:zeroinsert}. The number $\kappa^\circ_{r-1}:=\E^\circ_r(1/(r-1))$
is the critical excursion value of the uniform zero-free walk on the $r-1$
nonzero steps, so the second identity reads
$\kappa_r/r=\kappa^\circ_{r-1}/(r-1)$. \textup{[write]} This agrees with the
leading terms: Theorem~\ref{fce:lg:thm:general}, applied to both walks, gives
$A_r(n)\sim\frac{\kappa_r}{rn}\frac{(rn)!}{(n!)^r}$ and
$A^\circ_r(n)\sim\frac{\kappa^\circ_{r-1}}{(r-1)n}\frac{((r-1)n)!}{(n!)^{r-1}}$,
and \eqref{fce:dx:eq:zerobal} turns the second into the first exactly when
$\kappa_r/r=\kappa^\circ_{r-1}/(r-1)$. For $r=3$, $\kappa^\circ_2=2$ (Dyck
paths) and $\kappa_3=3$; for $r=5$, $\kappa^\circ_4=4\varphi^{-2}$
(Proposition~\ref{fce:dx:prop:Bn}) and $\kappa_5=5\varphi^{-2}=\frac52(3-\sqrt5)$.
Source~101-90 adds a warning that applies to its own proof: the marked
extraction below retains the zero letter, so the factor $\binom{rn}n$ is
already included and must not be inserted a second time.

\subsection{Extreme-unmarked coordinates and their Hessian}\label{fce:dx:sec:hessian}
Let $s_2<\cdots<s_{r-1}$ be the interior steps (all steps other than $\pm h$),
$d=r-2$, and give them marks $z_j=e^{y_j}$ ($2\le j\le r-1$), leaving the
extreme steps $\pm h$ unmarked:
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:Pex}
 P_{\rm ex}(u;\boldsymbol y)=u^{-h}+u^h+\sum_{j=2}^{r-1}e^{y_j}u^{s_j}.
\end{equation}
Let $\E_{\rm ex}(t;\boldsymbol z)$ and $B_{\rm ex}(t;\boldsymbol z)$ count
nonnegative excursions and bridges with these weights. At length $N=rn$, the
extracted interior counts $n$ and the two linear conditions (length $rn$,
final height $0$) force both extreme counts to be $n$, because
$s_r-s_1=2h\ne0$. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:exact}
 A_r(n)=[t^{rn}z_2^n\cdots z_{r-1}^n]\,\E_{\rm ex}(t;\boldsymbol z),\qquad
 [t^{rn}z_2^n\cdots z_{r-1}^n]\,B_{\rm ex}(t;\boldsymbol z)=\frac{(rn)!}{(n!)^r}.
\end{equation}
Near $\boldsymbol y=0$ let $\tau_{\rm ex}(\boldsymbol y)$ solve
$\partial_uP_{\rm ex}=0$ with $\tau_{\rm ex}(0)=1$, and put
$\rho_{\rm ex}=1/P_{\rm ex}(\tau_{\rm ex};\boldsymbol y)$ (so $\rho_{\rm ex}(0)=1/r$),
$V(\boldsymbol y)=\tau_{\rm ex}^2\partial_u^2P_{\rm ex}/P_{\rm ex}$ at $u=\tau_{\rm ex}$,
and
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:phaseex}
 \Phi_{\rm ex}(\boldsymbol y)=\log P_{\rm ex}(\tau_{\rm ex}(\boldsymbol y);\boldsymbol y)
 -\log r-\frac1r\sum_{j=2}^{r-1}y_j .
\end{equation}
At equal marks
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:variance}
 V=\frac1r\sum_is_i^2=\begin{cases}(r^2-1)/12,&r\text{ odd},\\(r^2-1)/3,&r\text{ even},\end{cases}
\end{equation}
since $\frac2r\sum_{k=1}^hk^2=h(h+1)/3$ for $r=2h+1$, and
$\frac2r\sum_{k=1}^{r/2}(2k-1)^2=(r^2-1)/3$ for even $r$.

\begin{proposition}[Hessian in extreme-unmarked coordinates (source~101-90)]\label{fce:dx:prop:det}
$\Phi_{\rm ex}$ is stationary at $0$, and its Hessian there is
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:Hex}
 H_{\rm ex}=C_{\rm ex}-\frac{\boldsymbol v\boldsymbol v^{\mathsf T}}{V},\qquad
 C_{\rm ex}=\frac1rI_d-\frac1{r^2}\one\one^{\mathsf T},\qquad
 v_j=\frac{s_j}r ,
\end{equation}
the covariance of the marked step indicators after elimination of the height
under the uniform step law. It is positive definite, and
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:detex}
 \det H_{\rm ex}=\frac{4h^2}{r^rV}=\frac{12(r-1)}{r^r(r+1)}
 =(r-1)^2\det H,
\end{equation}
with $\det H$ of \eqref{fce:eq:detH}; for $r=2$ the dimension is $0$ and both
sides are $1$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof (source~101-90)]
Positivity: otherwise a nonzero linear combination of the marked indicators
would be an affine function of the height at every step; it vanishes at the
two unmarked extreme steps, which have different heights, so the affine
function is zero, and then every coefficient vanishes. Determinant:
$C_{\rm ex}$ has the eigenvalue $1/r$ on $\one^\perp$ and $2/r^2$ on $\one$, so
$\det C_{\rm ex}=2/r^{r-1}$; the interior steps are symmetric, so
$\boldsymbol v\perp\one$ and $C_{\rm ex}^{-1}\boldsymbol v=r\boldsymbol v$; and
$\sum_js_j^2=rV-2h^2$. The matrix determinant lemma gives
\[
 \det H_{\rm ex}=\det C_{\rm ex}\Bigl(1-\frac{r\,|\boldsymbol v|^2}{V}\Bigr)
 =\frac2{r^{r-1}}\cdot\frac{2h^2}{rV}=\frac{4h^2}{r^rV}.
\]
With \eqref{fce:dx:eq:variance}, $4h^2=(r-1)^2$ and $V=(r^2-1)/12$ for odd
$r$, and $4h^2=4(r-1)^2$ and $V=(r^2-1)/3$ for even $r$; both give
$12(r-1)/(r^r(r+1))$.
\end{proof}
\begin{proof}[Second proof of \eqref{fce:dx:eq:detex}, with the comparison \textup{[write]}]
Follow the proof of Lemma~\ref{fce:lem:hessian}. Let $X$ take the value
$(\boldsymbol1_{\{i=2\}},\ldots,\boldsymbol1_{\{i=r-1\}},s_i)$ with
probability $1/r$. Its covariance matrix has Schur complement $H_{\rm ex}$
in the last entry $V$, so its determinant is $V\det H_{\rm ex}$. Its $r$
support points are affinely independent in dimension $r-1$, so that
determinant is $r^{-r}$ times the square of the affine-simplex determinant,
$\det\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}1&x_i\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)_{i=1}^r$. Expanding
along the interior rows, which carry the unit indicator vectors, leaves
$\pm\det\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}1&-h\\1&h\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)=\pm2h$.
Hence $\det H_{\rm ex}=4h^2/(r^rV)$. The same argument with the top adjacent
pair unmarked gives \eqref{fce:eq:detH}, with $\delta=s_r-s_{r-1}$ in place
of $2h=s_r-s_1$. So $\det H_{\rm ex}/\det H=(2h/\delta)^2$, and $2h/\delta=r-1$
in both parities ($2h=r-1$, $\delta=1$ for odd $r$; $2h=2(r-1)$, $\delta=2$
for even $r$).
\end{proof}
For $r=4$ and $r=5$, $\det H_{\rm ex}=9/320$ and $8/3125$, against
$\det H_4=1/320$ (Part~V) and $\det H=1/6250$ (Section~\ref{fce:sec:correction}).
Source~101-90's program checks \eqref{fce:dx:eq:detex} exactly for
$2\le r\le20$; the intake rechecked it for $2\le r\le12$.

\subsection{The full torus and the exact periodicity factor}\label{fce:dx:sec:torus}
\begin{lemma}[Dominant torus points (source~101-90)]\label{fce:dx:lem:torus}
Let $|z_j|=|u|=1$ and $|t|=1/r$, and write $u=e^{i\phi_u}$,
$t=e^{i\phi_t}/r$, $z_j=e^{i\theta_j}$. The kernel
$u^h(1-tP_{\rm ex}(u;\boldsymbol z))$ vanishes on $|u|=1$ exactly at
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:phases}
 \phi_u=\frac{\pi k}h,\qquad \phi_t=\pi k,\qquad
 \theta_j\equiv-(h+s_j)\phi_u\pmod{2\pi},\qquad 0\le k<2h.
\end{equation}
These are $2h/\nu=r-1$ distinct mark points, each with $\nu$ time phases.
At each of them the complete local expansion of the extraction
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:exact} equals that at $(t,\boldsymbol z)=(1/r,\one)$ term
by term, with phase factor one, so the total multiplicity is
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:copies}
 \nu(r-1)=2h .
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}[Proof (source~101-90, with the gauge computation written out \textup{[write]})]
A zero requires equality in $|t\sum_iz_iu^{s_i}|\le1$ (with $z_1=z_r=1$), so
$\phi_t+\theta_i+s_i\phi_u\equiv0$ for every $i$. The two unmarked extreme
steps give $\phi_t-h\phi_u\equiv\phi_t+h\phi_u\equiv0$, hence
$\phi_u=\pi k/h$ and $\phi_t=\pi k$, and then
$\theta_j\equiv-\phi_t-s_j\phi_u=-(h+s_j)\phi_u$. Two values $k,k'$ give the
same marks if and only if $2h$ divides $(k-k')(h+s_j)$ for every interior $j$. The
$\gcd$ of the integers $h+s_j$ is $1$ for odd $r$ (it contains
$h+(1-h)=1$) and $2$ for even $r$ (they are $2,4,\ldots,2h-2$), which is
$\nu$; so there are $2h/\nu$ mark points, each reached by $\nu$ values of $k$
whose time phases differ by $\pi h\equiv\pi$ when $\nu=2$ (for $r=2$ there
are no marks: one mark point with $\nu=2$ time phases).

For the gauge identity, the weight of a step of type $i$ at such a point is
$e^{-i(\phi_t+s_i\phi_u)}$ (also for the extreme steps). Along a path of
length $N$ and final height $0$ the product of these weights is
$e^{-iN\phi_t}$, so
$\E_{\rm ex}(t;\boldsymbol z_k\boldsymbol w)=\E_{\rm ex}(te^{-i\phi_t};\boldsymbol w)$
identically, and likewise for $B_{\rm ex}$. In the Cauchy integral for
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:exact} near $\boldsymbol z_k$ the factor
$\boldsymbol z^{-n\one}$ contributes
$e^{in\sum_j(\phi_t+s_j\phi_u)}=e^{ind\phi_t}$, since the interior steps sum
to zero, and the time coefficient contributes $e^{-irn\phi_t}$. The total
phase is $e^{-2in\phi_t}=e^{-2\pi ink}=1$. Since the identity is exact, every
correction term is transported, not only the leading one.
\end{proof}
Away from small neighbourhoods of these finitely many points, compactness
gives $\epsilon>0$ such that the kernel has no zero on $|u|=1$ for
$|t|\le1/r+\epsilon$; Rouch\'e's theorem keeps $h$ roots inside, and their
product is analytic even through collisions inside that group (by contour
power sums and Newton's identities), so the excursion series and the bridge
constant-term contour are analytic in the larger disk, uniformly on the outer
mark arcs, and Cauchy's bound makes those coefficients exponentially smaller.
This is the argument of Section~6.4, in other coordinates. Near each dominant
point the positive Hessian $H_{\rm ex}$ gives a Gaussian saddle in $d$
variables, and the bridge saddle reproduces the multinomial coefficient
exactly (source~101-90's normalization check):
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:normcheck}
 \frac{2h}{(2\pi)^{(r-1)/2}\sqrt V\sqrt{\det H_{\rm ex}}}\,r^NN^{-(r-1)/2}\Big|_{N=rn}
 =\sqrt r\,(2\pi n)^{-(r-1)/2}r^{rn},
\end{equation}
because $2h/(\sqrt V\sqrt{\det H_{\rm ex}})=r^{r/2}$ by \eqref{fce:dx:eq:detex}.
The excursion amplitude differs by $\kappa_r/N=\kappa_r/(rn)$, which proves
\eqref{fce:eq:probability} and \eqref{fce:eq:constant} again, with all
even/odd and zero-step normalizations.

\begin{remark}[The count of dominant points depends on the coordinates \textup{[write]}]\label{fce:dx:rem:invariant}
Section~6.4 finds \emph{one} dominant mark point with $\nu$ time phases;
Lemma~\ref{fce:dx:lem:torus} finds $r-1$. Both are right: the count depends on
which two steps are left unmarked, and the Gaussian determinant changes by
exactly the compensating factor. Leave any two steps $s_a<s_b$ unmarked. The
proof of Lemma~\ref{fce:dx:lem:torus} gives $\phi_t\equiv-s_a\phi_u$ and
$(s_b-s_a)\phi_u\equiv0$, hence exactly $s_b-s_a$ dominant pairs (mark point,
time phase); the gauge phase is
$e^{-iN\phi_t+in\sum_{j\ne a,b}(\phi_t+s_j\phi_u)}
=e^{in(s_a-s_b)\phi_u}=1$ at $N=rn$, so all of them contribute equally. The
proof of \eqref{fce:dx:eq:detex} gives
$\det H_{a,b}=(s_b-s_a)^2r^{-r}/V$. Therefore
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:invariant}
 \frac{\#\{\text{dominant pairs}\}}{\sqrt{\det H_{a,b}}}
 =\sqrt{r^rV}\quad\text{for every choice of }a<b ,
\end{equation}
which is $\nu/\sqrt{\det H}$ in the coordinates of Section~\ref{fce:sec:saddle}
($s_b-s_a=\delta=\nu$) and $2h/\sqrt{\det H_{\rm ex}}$ here ($s_b-s_a=2h$).
The ``$r-1$ dominant mark points'' of source~101-90's abstract are a property
of its coordinates, not of the problem.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The local saddle, the coefficient rule, and D-finiteness}\label{fce:dx:sec:recipe}
The weighted kernel formula \cite[Theorem~2]{bf} and the bridge identity
\cite[Corollary~2]{bf} are Lemmas~\ref{fce:lem:kernel} and~\ref{fce:lem:bridge}:
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:product}
 \E_{\rm ex}(t;\boldsymbol z)=\frac{(-1)^{h-1}}t\prod_{j=1}^hu_j(t;\boldsymbol z),
 \qquad B_{\rm ex}=1+t\partial_t\log\E_{\rm ex},
\end{equation}
with $u_j$ the small roots of $u^h(1-tP_{\rm ex})$. Source~101-90 rechecks the
bridge identity by the residue computation of source~61's first proof. The
coalescing small root is
$\tau_{\rm ex}-\tau_{\rm ex}\sqrt{2/V}\sqrt{1-t/\rho_{\rm ex}}+O(1-t/\rho_{\rm ex})$
and the product of the other inside roots is analytic, so
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:local}
 \E_{\rm ex}=\kappa_{\rm ex}(\boldsymbol y)\Bigl(1-\sqrt{2/V(\boldsymbol y)}\sqrt{1-t/\rho_{\rm ex}(\boldsymbol y)}
 +O(1-t/\rho_{\rm ex})\Bigr),\qquad
 \kappa_{\rm ex}(\boldsymbol y)=\E_{\rm ex}(\rho_{\rm ex}(\boldsymbol y);\boldsymbol y),
\end{equation}
and the bridge identity gives the bridge singularity
$1/\sqrt{2V(1-t/\rho_{\rm ex})}$. At one critical time the coefficient
amplitudes are therefore
$\rho_{\rm ex}^{-N}N^{-3/2}\kappa_{\rm ex}/\sqrt{2\pi V}$ for excursions and
$\rho_{\rm ex}^{-N}N^{-1/2}/\sqrt{2\pi V}$ for bridges, uniformly for complex
marks near one, before the copies of \eqref{fce:dx:eq:copies} are summed.

\emph{All orders.} The equal-mark critical kernel factors into the double
factor $(u-1)^2$ and its inside and outside factors, which are pairwise
coprime even if a stable group has repeated roots. Finite Hensel lifting
(equivalently, the implicit equations for the coefficients of the three
factors) produces their Taylor jets in time and marks and never divides by
differences of roots in the same group; this is the analytic factorization of
Section~\ref{fce:subsec:allorders}. With $t=\rho_{\rm ex}(1-x^2)$ and the
branch whose first displacement is $-\tau_{\rm ex}\sqrt{2/V}\,x$,
$\E_{\rm ex}=\sum_{k\ge0}\mathfrak e_k(\boldsymbol y)(1-t/\rho_{\rm ex})^{k/2}$,
and uniform transfer gives the single-time expansion
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:transfer}
 E^{\rm loc}_N(\boldsymbol y)=\rho_{\rm ex}(\boldsymbol y)^{-N}N^{-3/2}
 \Bigl(\sum_{s=0}^J\mathcal G_s(\boldsymbol y)N^{-s}+O_{r,J}(N^{-J-1})\Bigr),\quad
 \mathcal G_s=\sum_{k=0}^s\frac{\mathfrak e_{2k+1}}{\Gamma(-k-\frac12)}t_{s-k}(k+\tfrac12),
\end{equation}
with $t_j$ of \eqref{fce:lg:eq:transfer}; the time copies are summed
afterwards. Let $\Phi_{{\rm ex},\ell}$ be the homogeneous degree-$\ell$ part of
$\Phi_{\rm ex}$ and $G$ a centred Gaussian vector with covariance
$H_{\rm ex}^{-1}$. Source~101-90's coefficient rule is
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:recipe}
 \mathsf b_{r,j}=[x^{2j}]\,\Exp\Bigl[\sum_{s=0}^jx^{2s}
 \frac{\mathcal G_s(ixG)}{\mathcal G_0(0)}
 \exp\Bigl(\sum_{\ell=3}^{2j+2}i^\ell x^{\ell-2}\Phi_{{\rm ex},\ell}(G)\Bigr)\Bigr],
 \qquad \alpha_{r,j}=r^{-j}\mathsf b_{r,j},
\end{equation}
where $\mathcal G_s(ixG)$ is the amplitude at the log marks
$\boldsymbol y=ixG$, and the Gaussian expectation of a polynomial $F$ is
$\bigl[\exp\bigl(\frac12\sum_{a,b}(H_{\rm ex}^{-1})_{ab}\partial_a\partial_b\bigr)F\bigr](0)$.
Every letter, including a zero step, is retained, so $N=rn$ is the only
rescaling: no Stirling or zero-insertion factor is multiplied in. This is
source~42's rule \eqref{fce:lg:eq:wickseries}--\eqref{fce:lg:eq:betas}
($\mathsf b_{r,j}=\chi_j/\mathcal G_0(0)$) in marker instead of lattice
coordinates. Effective algebraicity follows as in
Section~\ref{fce:subsec:allorders}: the root factors have algebraic
coefficients, the phase jets and the covariance are rational, and the common
$\sqrt\pi$ factors cancel. For the remainder, restrict the angular marks to
radius $N^{-1/2+\varepsilon}$; Taylor remainders have a
polynomial-times-Gaussian majorant, the rest of the local ball contributes
$O(e^{-cN^{2\varepsilon}})$, the outer torus is exponentially small, and odd
half-orders vanish under Gaussian integration; the gauge identity of
Lemma~\ref{fce:dx:lem:torus} identifies all periodic contributions at every
order, so no residue class of $n$ is missed.

For $r=2,3$ the rule is checked by source~101-90's program from the Catalan
and marked Motzkin kernels:
$(\alpha_{2,1},\alpha_{2,2})=(-9/8,145/128)$ and
$(\alpha_{3,1},\alpha_{3,2})=(-11/9,101/81)$, the values of
\eqref{fce:lg:eq:r23corr} (source~42), and also those read off the closed forms
$A_2(n)=\binom{2n}n/(n+1)$, $A_3(n)=(3n)!/((n!)^3(n+1))$.

\begin{remark}[The rule at $r=4,5$, checked at this write \textup{[write]}]\label{fce:dx:rem:recipecheck}
Source~101-90's package exercises \eqref{fce:dx:eq:recipe} only in dimension
$d\le1$. At this write the rule was evaluated at $j=1$ for $r=2,3,4,5$ in
extreme-unmarked coordinates, numerically: kernel roots by
$40$-digit polynomial root finding, the coefficients in $x$ by Cauchy
integrals on small circles, and the Gaussian expectation by a four-point
Gauss--Hermite product rule, which is exact for the polynomial of degree at
most six that occurs. It reproduces $\alpha_{2,1}$ and $\alpha_{3,1}$, and
\[
 r^{-1}\mathsf b_{4,1}=-0.50978468486767535307\ldots,\qquad
 r^{-1}\mathsf b_{5,1}=-0.71862232585005467893\ldots,
\]
equal to $\alpha_{4,1}$ of Theorem~\ref{fce:ba:thm:alpha41} and to
$\alpha_{5,1}$ of \eqref{fce:eq:fivecorrection} to $33$ and $34$ digits. This
is numerical evidence that the rule as printed (the factor $r^{-j}$, the
multiplicity \eqref{fce:dx:eq:copies}, the Hessian \eqref{fce:dx:eq:Hex}) is
normalized correctly in dimensions $2$ and $3$; it is not an interval
certificate, and the proof of the rule is the one above.
\end{remark}

\emph{D-finiteness by an $r$-section.} By \eqref{fce:dx:eq:product},
$\E_{\rm ex}$ is algebraic. To write the slope-$r$ extraction
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:exact} as an ordinary diagonal, source~101-90 first takes the
$r$-section in time,
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:rsection}
 F^{(r)}(T;\boldsymbol z)=\sum_{N\ge0}\bigl([t^{rN}]\E_{\rm ex}\bigr)T^N
 =\frac1r\sum_{j=0}^{r-1}\E_{\rm ex}\bigl(e^{2\pi ij/r}T^{1/r};\boldsymbol z\bigr),
\end{equation}
a finite sum of algebraic functions with integer powers of $T$, hence
algebraic over $\Q(T,\boldsymbol z)$; then
$A_r(n)=[T^nz_2^n\cdots z_{r-1}^n]F^{(r)}$ is an ordinary diagonal, D-finite
by Lipshitz's theorem \cite{lipshitz}. This is a second route to the
D-finiteness of Theorem~\ref{fce:thm:class}, which takes the full diagonal of
the $r$-variable series (and which source~101-90 mentions as an
alternative); the proof of Corollary~\ref{fce:cor:rays} uses sections in the
same way. It proves existence of a recurrence, not a particular or minimal
one. Source~101-90 makes no transcendence statement.

\subsection{Inversion for every row}\label{fce:dx:sec:inverse90}
With $\lambda=r\log r$, $\beta=(r+1)/2$ and $x_0(Y)$ the Lambert core
\eqref{fce:eq:lambertcore}, source~101-90 gives, for every $r$,
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:inverse}
 x(Y)=x_0-\frac{\alpha_{r,1}}{\lambda x_0-\beta}
 -\frac{b_2}{x_0(\lambda x_0-\beta)}+O_r(x_0^{-3}),\qquad
 b_2=\alpha_{r,2}-\tfrac12\alpha_{r,1}^2 ,
\end{equation}
checked by substitution into $\log K_r+\lambda x-\beta\log x+\alpha_{r,1}/x+b_2/x^2$,
which leaves a residual $O_r(x_0^{-3})$; its program verifies this
symbolically for arbitrary $\beta$. \textup{[write]} Expanding
$1/(\lambda x_0-\beta)=(\lambda x_0)^{-1}(1+\beta/(\lambda x_0)+O(x_0^{-2}))$
turns \eqref{fce:dx:eq:inverse} into \eqref{fce:eq:inversefirst} exactly, so
this is the inversion of Section~\ref{fce:sec:inverse}; for $r=5$ it is
\eqref{fce:kk:eq:inversefive}, and its nested-logarithm form is
Remark~\ref{fce:ba:rem:inverse}. Further terms are recursive, each with the
fixed-order error inherited from the forward expansion. For the threshold
$N_r(Y)=\min\{n:A_r(n)\ge Y\}$ source~101-90 states
$N_r(Y)=\lceil x_J(Y)+\epsilon_J(Y)\rceil$ with
$\epsilon_J(Y)=O_{r,J}((\log Y)^{-J-1})$, where $x_J$ is the large solution
of the positive $J$-term approximation; ceilings of $x_J$ plus and minus an
error bound give rigorous bounds, a single ceiling is certified only when that
bracket avoids the integers, and ``no unqualified rule is asserted inside the
boundary windows''. This is \eqref{fce:lg:eq:rounding}, an instance of
\texttt{p0:thm:staircase} (Section~\ref{fce:sec:repository}), and its first
consequence
$N_r(Y)=(\log Y+\beta\log\log Y-\log K_r-\beta\log\lambda)/\lambda+O_r(1)$ is
\eqref{fce:lg:eq:loginverse}. No novelty is claimed.

\section{The five-letter row with the zero steps deleted (source~101-86)}\label{fce:dx:sec:five}
\subsection{Two marks on the zero-free walk}\label{fce:dx:sec:marks}
Map a letter $i$ to the step $3-i$; the words counted by $A_5(n)$ become
nonnegative excursions with $n$ steps of each of $-2,\ldots,2$. Deleting the
zero steps and choosing their positions gives
\eqref{fce:kk:eq:zeroinsert}, $A_5(n)=\binom{5n}nA^\circ_5(n)$, where the
$\binom{5n}n$ choices include positions before the first and after the last
nonzero step. Source~101-86 then marks two statistics of a zero-free
excursion: the number $M_2$ of steps $\pm2$ (mark $z_A=e^{y_A}$) and the
signed difference $D_2=\#(+2)-\#(-2)$ (mark $z_D=e^{y_D}$). With
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:Pcirc}
 P_\circ(u;z_A,z_D)=u+u^{-1}+z_A\bigl(z_Du^2+z_D^{-1}u^{-2}\bigr),\qquad
 A^\circ_5(n)=[t^{4n}z_A^{2n}z_D^0]\,\E_\circ(t;z_A,z_D),
\end{equation}
length $4n$, $M_2=2n$, $D_2=0$ and final height $0$ force $n$ copies of each
nonzero step. The kernel formula \cite[Theorem~2 and~(20)]{bf} gives
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:kernelcirc}
 \E_\circ=-\frac{u_1(t)u_2(t)}{tz_A/z_D},\qquad
 u^2-t\bigl(z_A/z_D+u+u^3+z_Az_Du^4\bigr)=0,
\end{equation}
with $u_1,u_2$ the small roots. Near $z_A=z_D=1$ let $\tau_\circ$ solve
$\partial_uP_\circ=0$ with $\tau_\circ(1,1)=1$ (the implicit function theorem
applies since $\partial_u^2P_\circ(1;1,1)=10$), and
$\rho_\circ=1/P_\circ(\tau_\circ)$. At equal marks
\[
 u^2-\tfrac14(1+u+u^3+u^4)=-\tfrac14(u-1)^2(u^2+3u+1),
\]
so $\rho_\circ=1/4$, the other small root is $\zeta=-\varphi^{-2}$ and the
remaining root is $-\varphi^2$. The critical equation
$u^3\partial_uP_\circ=0$ factors as $(u-1)(u+1)(2u^2+u+2)=0$; $u=-1$ has
$P_\circ=0$ and no finite critical time, and $2u^2+u+2=0$ gives the only
other finite critical value $t=-4/9$. The square-root singularity at $\rho_\circ$
is therefore isolated uniformly for marks in a small complex neighbourhood
of $(1,1)$; source~101-86 takes ``a fixed outer radius between $1/4$ and
$4/9$, with a slit from the moving critical value'' as the common transfer
domain (a sketched step; the full argument of
Section~\ref{fce:subsec:allorders} covers it). With
$V_\circ=\tau_\circ^2\partial_u^2P_\circ/P_\circ$ and
$\kappa_\circ=-\tau_\circ\zeta/(\rho_\circ z_A/z_D)$,
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:A0}
 V_\circ=\frac52,\qquad \kappa_\circ=4\varphi^{-2},\qquad
 \mathcal G_0(1,1)=\frac{4\varphi^{-2}}{\sqrt{5\pi}}
\end{equation}
at equal marks, and \eqref{fce:dx:eq:transfer} holds with $\rho_\circ$,
$\tau_\circ$, $V_\circ$, analytic amplitudes $\mathcal G_j(z_A,z_D)$, and
positive branches at the equal-mark point.

\emph{The torus.} For $|z_A|=|z_D|=|u|=1$ and $|t|=1/4$, with angles
$\theta_A,\theta_D,\phi_u,\phi_t$, a kernel zero on $|u|=1$ requires the four
phases $\phi_t-\phi_u$, $\phi_t+\phi_u$, $\phi_t+\theta_A-\theta_D-2\phi_u$,
$\phi_t+\theta_A+\theta_D+2\phi_u$ to vanish modulo $2\pi$, so the only mark
points are
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:four}
 (z_A,z_D)\in\{(1,1),(-1,-1),(-1,1),(1,-1)\}.
\end{equation}
Away from them, compactness, Rouch\'e's theorem (exactly two roots inside)
and the analytic product of those two roots (by contour power sums and
Newton's identities, through collisions) give an exponentially smaller
contribution; the singularity of \eqref{fce:dx:eq:kernelcirc} at $t=0$ is
removable. For every zero-free excursion $N-M_2$ and $M_2-D_2$ are even, so
$\E_N(-z_A,z_D)=\E_N(z_A,-z_D)=(-1)^N\E_N(z_A,z_D)$ for the length-$N$
coefficients; at $N=4n$ and $z_A$-degree $2n$ the four neighbourhoods
contribute equally with positive phase. Source~101-86 calls the resulting
factor $4$ the ``lattice factor''. It is the index of the sublattice carrying
$(N,M_2,D_2)$, a property of these coordinates
(Remarks~\ref{fce:dx:rem:invariant} and~\ref{fce:dx:rem:lattice}).

\subsection{The leading constant of the zero-free count}\label{fce:dx:sec:Bn}
\begin{proposition}[The constant of $A^\circ_5(n)$ (source~101-86)]\label{fce:dx:prop:Bn}
Under the uniform law on the steps $-2,-1,1,2$, $\Var M_2=\frac14$,
$\Var D_2=\frac12$, $\Var(\text{height})=\frac52$,
$\Cov(D_2,\text{height})=1$, and the other covariances vanish (per step).
The Hessian of
$\Phi_\circ(y_A,y_D)=\log P_\circ(\tau_\circ;e^{y_A},e^{y_D})-\log4-y_A/2$
at $0$ is therefore
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:Hcirc}
 H_\circ=\operatorname{diag}\bigl(\tfrac14,\tfrac1{10}\bigr),\qquad \det H_\circ=\frac1{40},
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:Bleading}
 A^\circ_5(n)=\frac{4\mathcal G_0(1,1)}{2\pi\sqrt{\det H_\circ}}\,4^{4n}(4n)^{-5/2}\bigl(1+O(n^{-1})\bigr)
 =\frac{\varphi^{-2}\,256^n}{\sqrt2\,\pi^{3/2}n^{5/2}}\bigl(1+O(n^{-1})\bigr).
\end{equation}
With
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:binom}
 \binom{5n}n=\frac{\sqrt5}{2\sqrt{2\pi n}}\Bigl(\frac{3125}{256}\Bigr)^n
 \Bigl(1-\frac7{80n}+O(n^{-2})\Bigr),
\end{equation}
this gives the leading constant $K_5=(3\sqrt5-5)/(8\pi^2)$ of
\eqref{fce:eq:fivecorrection}.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The covariances are elementary, and eliminating the height (the Schur
complement in its entry $5/2$) changes only $\Var D_2$, to
$\frac12-1/\frac52=\frac1{10}$; source~101-86 notes that this is also the
implicit differentiation of $\partial_uP_\circ=0$. The real part of the phase
on angular marks has a strict Gaussian maximum, its quadratic bound controls
the intermediate arcs and the torus argument above the rest, so the factor
$4$, the Gaussian integral in two variables and \eqref{fce:dx:eq:A0} give
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:Bleading}; Stirling's formula gives \eqref{fce:dx:eq:binom}.
\end{proof}
\textup{[write]} Three consistency checks. (i) For the uniform zero-free walk,
Theorem~\ref{fce:lg:thm:general} (source~42) gives
$A^\circ_5(n)\sim\frac{\kappa^\circ_4}{4n}\frac{(4n)!}{(n!)^4}$, with
$\kappa^\circ_4$ its critical excursion value; the small-root formula at the
critical weights, as in Proposition~\ref{fce:prop:kappa}, gives
$\kappa^\circ_4=(-1)^{3}\cdot4\cdot(-\varphi^{-2})=4\varphi^{-2}=\kappa_\circ$,
and $(4n)!/(n!)^4\sim2\cdot256^n(2\pi n)^{-3/2}$ turns this into
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:Bleading}. (ii) Dividing $K_55^{5n}n^{-3}$ by
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:binom} gives the same constant,
$K_5\cdot2\sqrt{2\pi}/\sqrt5=\varphi^{-2}/(\sqrt2\pi^{3/2})=0.0485048217\ldots$.
(iii) Proposition~\ref{fce:dx:prop:zero} gives $\kappa_5=\frac54\kappa^\circ_4$.
The ratio of $A^\circ_5(n)$ (source~101-86's shipped enumeration) to the right
side of \eqref{fce:dx:eq:Bleading} is $0.93936$, $0.96907$, $0.97924$ at
$n=10,20,30$. The constant of $A^\circ_5(n)$ was not printed before in this
report (Part~IV reaches $A^\circ_5(n)$ only through $\Lambda_n$). It has the
shape of Theorem~\ref{fce:ba:thm:alpha41}'s leading term for A215562, with
$\varrho=\kappa_4/4$ replaced by $\varphi^{-2}=\kappa^\circ_4/4$: both count
balanced excursions of a four-step walk, and Theorem~\ref{fce:lg:thm:general}
gives every such count the form
$\frac\kappa4\,256^n/(\sqrt2\,\pi^{3/2}n^{5/2})$.

\subsection{The coefficient rule in these coordinates}\label{fce:dx:sec:recipe5}
Let $\Phi_{\circ,\ell}$ be the homogeneous degree-$\ell$ part of $\Phi_\circ$ and
$G=(G_A,G_D)$ a centred Gaussian with covariance
$H_\circ^{-1}=\operatorname{diag}(4,10)$. With
$\E_\circ=\sum_{k\ge0}\mathfrak e_k(z_A,z_D)(1-t/\rho_\circ)^{k/2}$, the
amplitudes $\mathcal G_s$ are given by \eqref{fce:dx:eq:transfer}. Source~101-86
sets $\mathsf b^\circ_0=1$ and
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:recipe5}
 \mathsf b^\circ_j=[x^{2j}]\,\Exp\Bigl[\sum_{s=0}^jx^{2s}
 \frac{\mathcal G_s(e^{ixG_A},e^{ixG_D})}{\mathcal G_0(1,1)}
 \exp\Bigl(\sum_{\ell=3}^{2j+2}i^\ell x^{\ell-2}\Phi_{\circ,\ell}(G)\Bigr)\Bigr],
\end{equation}
so that $A^\circ_5(n)$ is the right side of \eqref{fce:dx:eq:Bleading} times
$1+\sum_j\mathsf b^\circ_j(4n)^{-j}$. The binomial factor contributes
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:dj}
 \sum_{j\ge0}\mathfrak d_jw^j=\exp\Bigl[\sum_{k\ge1}\frac{B_{2k}}{2k(2k-1)}
 \bigl(5^{1-2k}-1-4^{1-2k}\bigr)w^{2k-1}\Bigr],
\end{equation}
with Bernoulli numbers $B_{2k}$ (Stirling's series for
$\log(5n)!-\log n!-\log(4n)!$); so $\mathfrak d_1=-7/80$ and
$\mathfrak d_2=\mathfrak d_1^2/2=49/12800$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:cj}
 \alpha_{5,j}=\sum_{s=0}^j\mathfrak d_{j-s}\,\frac{\mathsf b^\circ_s}{4^s}.
\end{equation}
Only the Taylor coefficients able to reach degree $2j$ are needed; the
$\mathfrak e_k$ follow by finite algebraic substitution in the quartic
kernel. All Gaussian moments are rational and all normalized root jets at the
base point lie in $\Q(\sqrt5)$, so \eqref{fce:dx:eq:recipe5}--\eqref{fce:dx:eq:cj}
give $\alpha_{5,j}\in\Q(\sqrt5)$ for every $j$, the field statement of
Theorem~\ref{fce:thm:five} by a second route. For the remainder,
source~101-86 retains finitely many terms of the uniform Puiseux transfer,
Taylor-expands amplitudes and phase in a neighbourhood of radius
$N^{-1/2+\epsilon}$, bounds the Taylor remainders by a polynomial times a
strictly weaker Gaussian and the complement by $O(e^{-cN^{2\epsilon}})$
locally and exponentially small globally; ``no exponentially improved error
is asserted''.

\subsection{The second correction without the recurrence}\label{fce:dx:sec:alpha52}
\begin{theorem}[Two corrections for A215570 by Gaussian moments (source~101-86)]\label{fce:dx:thm:alpha52}
The rule \eqref{fce:dx:eq:recipe5}--\eqref{fce:dx:eq:cj}, evaluated in exact
arithmetic in $\Q(\sqrt5)$, gives
\begin{align}
 \mathsf b^\circ_1&=-\frac{97}{20}+\frac{26\sqrt5}{25},&
 \mathsf b^\circ_2&=\frac{681}{32}-\frac{77\sqrt5}{10},\notag\\
 \alpha_{5,1}&=\frac{13(\sqrt5-5)}{50},&
 \alpha_{5,2}&=\frac{180-63\sqrt5}{125}=\frac{36}{25}-\frac{63\sqrt5}{125}.
 \label{fce:dx:eq:alpha52}
\end{align}
The recurrence of Theorem~\ref{fce:kk:thm:recurrence} is not used.
\end{theorem}
The ingredients follow; they are source~101-86's Section~5 together with the
complete jets its program records (\path{data/101-86-five-second_correction.json}),
which the theorem needs and the manuscript only names. Write $w=\log\tau_\circ$.
The critical equation and the step polynomial are
\[
 2\sinh w+4e^{y_A}\sinh(y_D+2w)=0,\qquad
 P_\circ=2\cosh w+2e^{y_A}\cosh(y_D+2w),
\]
and their solution begins
\[
 w=-\tfrac25y_D-\tfrac2{25}y_Ay_D+\tfrac3{125}y_A^2y_D+\tfrac1{625}y_D^3
 -\tfrac1{1875}y_A^3y_D+\tfrac7{9375}y_Ay_D^3+O(|\boldsymbol y|^5).
\]
\emph{Phase.} The homogeneous parts of $\Phi_\circ$ are
\begin{align}\label{fce:dx:eq:phasejets}
 \Phi_{\circ,2}&=\tfrac18y_A^2+\tfrac1{20}y_D^2,\qquad
 \Phi_{\circ,3}=-\tfrac3{200}y_Ay_D^2,\qquad
 \Phi_{\circ,4}=-\tfrac1{192}y_A^4-\tfrac1{125}y_A^2y_D^2-\tfrac{41}{60000}y_D^4,\notag\\
 \Phi_{\circ,5}&=\tfrac1{100000}y_Ay_D^2\bigl(365y_A^2+82y_D^2\bigr),\notag\\
 \Phi_{\circ,6}&=\tfrac1{2880}y_A^6+\tfrac{23}{37500}y_A^4y_D^2
 +\tfrac{217}{1200000}y_A^2y_D^4+\tfrac{293}{22500000}y_D^6 ;
\end{align}
in derivatives, $\Phi_{aaa}=0$, $\Phi_{abb}=-\frac3{100}$,
$\Phi_{aaaa}=-\frac18$, $\Phi_{aabb}=-\frac4{125}$, $\Phi_{bbbb}=-\frac{41}{2500}$
($a=y_A$, $b=y_D$).

\emph{Amplitude.} Source~101-86 computes the amplitude ``particularly
economically'' from
\[
 \log\mathcal G_0=\log P_\circ-y_A-\operatorname{arcosh}(c_0)-\tfrac12\log V_\circ-\tfrac12\log(2\pi),
 \qquad c_0=\cosh(y_D+2w)+\tfrac12e^{-y_A}\cosh w,
\]
which follows from the remaining small root:
$\tau_\circ\zeta z_D=-\exp[-\operatorname{arcosh}(c_0)]$ (at equal marks
$c_0=\frac32$ and $e^{-\operatorname{arcosh}(3/2)}=\varphi^{-2}$). The normalized
amplitude $\mathfrak a_\circ=\mathcal G_0(e^{y_A},e^{y_D})/\mathcal G_0(1,1)$ has
the homogeneous parts
\begin{align}\label{fce:dx:eq:ampjets}
 [\mathfrak a_\circ]_1&=\Bigl(\frac{\sqrt5}5-\frac{13}{20}\Bigr)y_A,\qquad
 [\mathfrak a_\circ]_2=\Bigl(\frac{367}{800}-\frac{17\sqrt5}{100}\Bigr)y_A^2
 +\Bigl(\frac{59}{1000}-\frac{3\sqrt5}{125}\Bigr)y_D^2,\notag\\
 [\mathfrak a_\circ]_3&=\frac{y_A}{240000}\Bigl((25700\sqrt5-57275)y_A^2+(8496\sqrt5-16908)y_D^2\Bigr),\\
 [\mathfrak a_\circ]_4&=\Bigl(\frac{81821}{768000}-\frac{2423\sqrt5}{48000}\Bigr)y_A^4
 +\Bigl(\frac{35273}{800000}-\frac{1353\sqrt5}{50000}\Bigr)y_A^2y_D^2
 +\Bigl(\frac{12517}{6000000}-\frac{23\sqrt5}{25000}\Bigr)y_D^4,\notag
\end{align}
that is, $\mathcal G_{0,a}/\mathcal G_0=1/\sqrt5-13/20$, $\mathcal G_{0,b}/\mathcal G_0=0$,
$\mathcal G_{0,aa}/\mathcal G_0=367/400-17\sqrt5/50$ and
$\mathcal G_{0,bb}/\mathcal G_0=59/500-6\sqrt5/125$.

\emph{Transfer at equal marks.} Put $t=(1-x^2)/4$ and
$v_\pm=\bigl(-1\pm\sqrt{9+4/t}\bigr)/2$ (the values of $u+u^{-1}$ at the roots
of the kernel). The small roots are $(v_+-\sqrt{v_+^2-4})/2$ and
$(v_-+\sqrt{v_-^2-4})/2$, and the odd Puiseux coefficients of
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:kernelcirc} are
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:puiseux5}
 \mathfrak e_1=4-\frac{12\sqrt5}5,\qquad
 \mathfrak e_3=8-\frac{536\sqrt5}{125},\qquad
 \mathfrak e_5=12-\frac{19108\sqrt5}{3125}.
\end{equation}
By \eqref{fce:dx:eq:transfer}, with $t_1(\frac12)=\frac38$,
$t_2(\frac12)=\frac{25}{128}$, $t_1(\frac32)=\frac{15}8$ and the gamma ratios
$-\frac32$, $\frac{15}4$,
\begin{align}\label{fce:dx:eq:G1G2}
 \frac{\mathcal G_1}{\mathcal G_0}(1,1)&=\frac38-\frac{3\mathfrak e_3}{2\mathfrak e_1}=-\frac{381-48\sqrt5}{200},\\
 \frac{\mathcal G_2}{\mathcal G_0}(1,1)&=\frac{25}{128}-\frac{45\mathfrak e_3}{16\mathfrak e_1}
 +\frac{15\mathfrak e_5}{4\mathfrak e_1}=\frac{53669}{16000}-\frac{411\sqrt5}{500}.\notag
\end{align}
\emph{The marked transfer correction.} The second-order rule needs
$\mathcal G_1/\mathcal G_0$ to degree two in the marks. With
$\mathcal V_j=(u\partial_u)^jP_\circ/P_\circ$ at $u=\tau_\circ$, expand the
coalescing root as $\tau_\circ\exp(\varkappa_1x+\varkappa_2x^2+\varkappa_3x^3+\cdots)$;
then
\[
 \varkappa_1^2=\frac2{\mathcal V_2},\qquad
 \varkappa_2=-\frac{\mathcal V_3}{3\mathcal V_2^2},\qquad
 \frac{\varkappa_3}{\varkappa_1}=\frac12+\frac{5\mathcal V_3^2}{36\mathcal V_2^3}-\frac{\mathcal V_4}{12\mathcal V_2^2},
\]
and implicit differentiation of $tP_\circ(\zeta(t))=1$ supplies the other small
root, so that, as functions of the marks,
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:R3}
 \frac{\mathfrak e_3}{\mathfrak e_1}=\frac32+\frac{5\mathcal V_3^2}{36\mathcal V_2^3}
 -\frac{\mathcal V_4}{12\mathcal V_2^2}-\frac{\mathcal V_3}{3\mathcal V_2^2}
 +\frac1{3\mathcal V_2}+\frac{P_\circ(\tau_\circ)}{\zeta\,\partial_uP_\circ(\zeta)} .
\end{equation}
This gives
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:G1jet}
 \frac{\mathcal G_1}{\mathcal G_0}=-\frac{381}{200}+\frac{6\sqrt5}{25}
 +\Bigl(\frac{33}{1000}+\frac{9\sqrt5}{125}\Bigr)y_A
 +\Bigl(\frac{3\sqrt5}{250}-\frac{171}{10000}\Bigr)y_A^2
 -\Bigl(\frac{3\sqrt5}{250}+\frac{339}{50000}\Bigr)y_D^2+O(|\boldsymbol y|^3).
\end{equation}
\emph{First order.} The Gaussian contraction at $j=1$ is
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:b1}
 \mathsf b^\circ_1=\frac{\mathcal G_1}{\mathcal G_0}-2\frac{\mathcal G_{0,aa}}{\mathcal G_0}
 -5\frac{\mathcal G_{0,bb}}{\mathcal G_0}-\frac35\frac{\mathcal G_{0,a}}{\mathcal G_0}-\frac{91}{100}
 =-\frac{97}{20}+\frac{26\sqrt5}{25},
\end{equation}
where $-2\mathcal G_{0,aa}/\mathcal G_0-5\mathcal G_{0,bb}/\mathcal G_0$ is the
amplitude Hessian against $H_\circ^{-1}$, $\Exp\Phi_{\circ,4}(G)=-\frac{31}{40}$ is the
quartic phase term, $-\frac12\Exp\Phi_{\circ,3}(G)^2=-\frac{27}{200}$ the
squared cubic term (their sum is $-\frac{91}{100}$), and
$-\frac35\mathcal G_{0,a}/\mathcal G_0$ the amplitude--cubic cross term. Then
$\alpha_{5,1}=\mathsf b^\circ_1/4-\frac7{80}$ by \eqref{fce:dx:eq:cj}.

\emph{Second order.} At $j=2$, \eqref{fce:dx:eq:recipe5} needs the phase through
degree six \eqref{fce:dx:eq:phasejets}, the amplitude through degree four
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:ampjets}, $\mathcal G_1/\mathcal G_0$ through degree two
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:G1jet} and $\mathcal G_2/\mathcal G_0$ at equal marks
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:G1G2}. Gaussian integration, a finite exact computation that source~101-86's
program performs and that neither the manuscript nor this Part prints term by
term (every input is printed above), gives $\mathsf b^\circ_2$ of
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:alpha52}, and
\[
 \alpha_{5,2}=\frac{\mathsf b^\circ_2}{16}-\frac{7\mathsf b^\circ_1}{320}+\frac{49}{12800}
 =\frac{36}{25}-\frac{63\sqrt5}{125}.
\]
``This is finite exact arithmetic using the displayed coefficient rule; the
sequence recurrence is not involved.''

\textup{[write]} The last step is checked by hand: $\frac{681}{512}+\frac{679}{6400}
+\frac{49}{12800}=\frac{36}{25}$ and $-\frac{77}{160}-\frac{91}{4000}=-\frac{63}{125}$.
The value equals $\alpha_{5,2}$ of Proposition~\ref{fce:kk:prop:alpha}, which
source~75-03 obtained from the recurrence of
Theorem~\ref{fce:kk:thm:recurrence} with $\alpha_{5,1}$ as an initial datum;
the two derivations are independent (source~101-86 recomputes $\alpha_{5,1}$
as well), and their agreement checks both. At the intake, the exact terms $A_5(n)$ of
Theorem~\ref{fce:kk:thm:coefficient} give
$n^3\bigl(A_5(n)/(K_55^{5n}n^{-3})-1-\alpha_{5,1}/n-\alpha_{5,2}/n^2\bigr)
=-0.09501,-0.09534,-0.09550,-0.09556$ at $n=50,100,200,300$, approaching
$\alpha_{5,3}=-0.0956673\ldots$ of Table~\ref{fce:kk:tab:alpha}. Source~101-86's
amplitude, phase and transfer jets were reconstructed by its second program
in another way (Section~\ref{fce:dx:sec:checks}).

\subsection{Inversion, and monotonicity at every index}\label{fce:dx:sec:monotone}
Source~101-86's inverse is the case $r=5$ of
Section~\ref{fce:dx:sec:inverse90}: $x_0(Y)=-\frac3\lambda
W_{-1}\bigl(-\frac\lambda3(K_5/Y)^{1/3}\bigr)$, $\lambda=5\log5$, and
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:inverse} with $b_2=(213-83\sqrt5)/500$ (its $\ell_2$), which
is \eqref{fce:kk:eq:inversefive}; since its $b_2$ comes from
Theorem~\ref{fce:dx:thm:alpha52}, this second-order inverse is now also
recurrence-free. Its rounded threshold
$N_5(Y)=\lceil x_J(Y)+\epsilon_J(Y)\rceil$, $\epsilon_J=O_J((\log Y)^{-J-1})$,
with the same qualification as in Section~\ref{fce:dx:sec:inverse90}, is
\eqref{fce:lg:eq:rounding}. New is the discrete input.

\begin{proposition}[Supermultiplicativity (source~101-86 for $m=1$, $r=5$; general form \textup{[write]})]\label{fce:dx:prop:supermult}
For every $r\ge2$ and $m,n\ge0$,
\begin{equation}\label{fce:dx:eq:supermult}
 A_r(m+n)\ge A_r(m)\,A_r(n);\qquad\text{in particular}\qquad
 A_5(n+1)\ge35\,A_5(n).
\end{equation}
For $r\ge3$, $A_r(1)\ge2$, so $A_r(n+1)\ge2A_r(n)>A_r(n)$ for every $n\ge0$;
for $r=2$ the Catalan numbers have $A_2(0)=A_2(1)=1$ and increase strictly
from $n=1$ on.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof \textup{[write; source~101-86's argument for one-copy words]}]
Let $w$ have content $n\one$ and $w'$ content $m\one$, both satisfying
\eqref{fce:eq:prefix}. The letters of $w$ sum to exactly $\frac{r+1}2rn$, so a
prefix of $ww'$ of length $rn+k$ ($0\le k\le rm$) has sum
$\frac{r+1}2rn+\sum_{j\le k}w'_j\le\frac{r+1}2(rn+k)$; shorter prefixes are
prefixes of $w$. So $ww'$ is counted by $A_r(m+n)$, and $w,w'$ are recovered
from $ww'$ by their lengths. For $r\ge3$ the words $12\cdots r$ and
$213\cdots r$ are counted by $A_r(1)$: their prefixes of length $k\ge2$ have
sum $k(k+1)/2\le\frac{r+1}2k$, and the first letter of the second is
$2\le\frac{r+1}2$. Finally $A_r(n)\ge1$ (the word $(12\cdots r)^n$), and
$A_5(1)=35$.
\end{proof}
Source~101-86 states only $A_5(n+1)\ge35A_5(n)$, by concatenation with any
one of the $35$ valid one-copy words. Section~\ref{fce:sec:thresholds}
appends one fixed block and concludes that $A_r(n)$ is nondecreasing and
\emph{eventually} strictly increasing (also source~101-90, and
Section~\ref{fce:lg:sec:inverse}); by
Proposition~\ref{fce:dx:prop:supermult} it is strictly increasing at every
index for $r\ge3$, with ratio at least $A_r(1)$ (the values
$A_r(1)=1,3,7,35,139,1001$ for $r=2,\ldots,7$), so the threshold $N_r(Y)$ needs
no tail qualification. \textup{[write]} By Fekete's lemma the
supermultiplicativity also gives $\lim A_r(n)^{1/n}=\sup_nA_r(n)^{1/n}$, which
is $r^r$ by Theorem~\ref{fce:thm:main}; this adds nothing beyond the trivial
bound $A_r(n)\le(rn)!/(n!)^r\le r^{rn}$. The enumerations of both packages
satisfy \eqref{fce:dx:eq:supermult} on their whole ranges ($r\le7$).

\section{Computations, the lattice-peak theorem, stale claims and questions}\label{fce:dx:sec:end}
\subsection{Source~101-86 and the lattice-peak transfer theorem}\label{fce:dx:sec:lattice}
\begin{remark}[Not an instance; dated October~5, 2026]\label{fce:dx:rem:lattice}
Part~II of the collection's report \emph{Theta Asymptotics for High-Power
Ballot Sums} (\path{SetTheory/Cardinals/docs/reports/generating-functions-and-asymptotics/oeis-sequence-asymptotics/a357825-theta-ballot-power-sums},
written in batch~98) proves a lattice-peak transfer theorem
(\texttt{tbs:lpt:thm:transfer}, Theorem~15.1 there, and its multidimensional
form \texttt{tbs:lpt:thm:multi}, Theorem~18.1): for power sums
$S_n(r_n)=\sum_xC_{n,x}^{r_n}$ of a row on a moving lattice of mesh $d_n$,
with growing exponents in the critical window
$q_n=r_nd_n^2/\sigma_n^2\in K\subset(0,\infty)$,
$S_n(r_n)/\Omega_n^{r_n}=\vartheta(q_n,\delta_n)+o(1)$, a theta factor that
depends on the lattice phase $\delta_n$. Source~101-86's ``lattice factor~$4$''
is \emph{not} an instance of it, for three reasons.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item There is no power sum and no growing exponent: $A^\circ_5(n)$ is one
coefficient, extracted by one Cauchy integral; in that theorem's terms
$r_n\equiv1$.
\item The factor counts the dominant points \eqref{fce:dx:eq:four} of the mark
torus, all of phase one by the parity identities; equivalently, it is the
index of the sublattice that carries the support of $(N,M_2,D_2)$, the
classical periodicity factor of a lattice local limit theorem. Read as a
lattice sum of a Gaussian, the extraction has a fixed mesh and a variance
growing like $n$, so $q\asymp1/n\to0$: the fine-lattice regime, where
Proposition~16.1 there (\texttt{tbs:lpt:prop:theta-regimes}) gives
$\vartheta(q,\delta)=\sqrt{2\pi/q}\,(1+O(e^{-2\pi^2/q}))$, independent of the
phase, and Remark~18.2 there (\texttt{tbs:lpt:rem:variants}) says to
parametrize such a sublattice first, which turns the factor into its index.
No theta oscillation arises, consistently with the complete, non-oscillating
expansion of Theorem~\ref{fce:thm:main}.
\item The factor depends on the coordinates: source~101-90's analogue is
$2h=\nu(r-1)$ (Lemma~\ref{fce:dx:lem:torus}), the coordinates of
Section~\ref{fce:sec:saddle} give $\nu$, and the combination
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:invariant} with the Gaussian determinant is the same in all
of them. \textup{[write]} For the zero-free walk with the steps $-2,-1$ marked
and $1,2$ unmarked, there is one dominant point and $\det=1/640$; source~101-86
has four points and $\det H_\circ=1/40=16/640$, and $4/\sqrt{1/40}=\sqrt{640}$.
\end{enumerate}
The same holds for source~101-90. That report's README lists source~101-86's
archive among later arrivals ``not examined'' for its write; this remark
records the examination.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The two packages}\label{fce:dx:sec:checks}
Source~101-90 ships four programs and a driver
(\path{code/101-90-alphabet-*}) with their recorded outputs
(\path{data/101-90-alphabet-*}). \path{check_constants.py} checks the
critical-multiplicity factorization and the inside-root count for
$2\le r\le10$, the exact values $\kappa_2,\ldots,\kappa_5$ and $c(4)$, $c(5)$,
and records numerical root products, which it labels orientation for $r\ge6$.
\path{check_balanced_counts.py} enumerates content vectors directly for
$r=2,\ldots,7$ up to $n=30,20,16,9,7,5$, against the Catalan and $r=3$ closed
forms and OEIS initial terms. \path{check_lattice.py} checks the phase classes
and gauge phases of Lemma~\ref{fce:dx:lem:torus}, the determinant
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:detex} and the bridge normalization exactly for $2\le r\le20$
(``PASS 19 alphabets''). \path{check_recipe_small.py} evaluates
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:recipe} at $j=1,2$ for $r=2,3$ and verifies
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:inverse} symbolically.

Source~101-86 ships six programs and a driver (\path{code/101-86-five-*}),
their outputs and a review constants file (\path{data/101-86-five-*}).
\path{check_kernel.py} checks the sextic over the coefficient box
$0\le n_i\le3$ ($1{,}024$ positions, $112$ nonzero excursion coefficients),
the diagonal $1,35,18720,19369350$ and the zero-step removal, with
$A^\circ_5(n)=1,7,416,42570$; \path{direct_terms.py} enumerates
$A^\circ_5(n)$ for $n\le30$ by dynamic programming ($467{,}976$ states, no
recurrence) and compares $\binom{5n}nA^\circ_5(n)$ with $13$ OEIS terms;
\path{check_jets.py} reconstructs the first-order jets and $\alpha_{5,1}$;
\path{check_second_correction.py} all the second-order jets of
Section~\ref{fce:dx:sec:alpha52} and $\alpha_{5,2}$ (about $100$~s recorded);
\path{check_auxiliary.py} the pair-product elimination of the sextic, the
second inverse correction and $b_2$; and
\path{audit-check_second_assembly_fast.py} (delivered as
\path{checks/audit/check_second_assembly_fast.py}) reconstructs the implicit
root, the phase, the amplitude (algebraically, without $\operatorname{arcosh}$
Taylor series) and the transfer jets by finite power-series convolution and
assembles the Gaussian contraction independently.

\emph{What the second program does not do.} Source~101-86's README calls it
the ``final independent check''. It reads the main checker's output
\path{second_correction.json}: it takes the implicit root jet from there and
only verifies it (the residual of the critical equation vanishes); it
recomputes the phase, amplitude and transfer jets by another method and
compares them with the recorded ones; and it then assembles
$\mathsf b^\circ_2$ and $\alpha_{5,2}$ from the recorded jets, which it has
just found equal to its own. It is therefore a verification of the main
computation, not a derivation independent of its output.

At the intake both suites were rerun on copies in their delivered layout
(Python~3.14.4, SymPy~1.14.0): source~101-86's six jobs passed (``All exact
checks passed''; $445$~s on a shared machine, against $131$~s recorded) and
source~101-90's four (``All checks passed''; $24$~s, against $12$~s). Their
regenerated outputs equal the shipped ones apart from line endings (three
files each) or the recorded running time (three and two files). At this write
the enumerated $A^\circ_5(n)$, $n\le30$, were found equal to
$A_5(n)/\binom{5n}n$ from Theorem~\ref{fce:kk:thm:coefficient}, and both
enumerations to satisfy \eqref{fce:dx:eq:supermult}.

Both packages state their scope: finite checks ``supplement the analytic
proof'' and use no guessed recurrence; source~101-90's code ``checks examples
of that procedure; it is not a general-purpose implementation''. Their review
files (\path{101-90-alphabet-structural_review.md},
\path{101-86-five-structural_review.md}) review drafts that were not delivered
(\path{leading_comparison.md} and \path{all_orders_and_inverse.md};
\path{asymptotic_proof.md}), and their QA receipts record approvals by the
producing pipeline. Source~101-86's \path{check_receipt.json} has a key,
\texttt{independent\_portable\_replay\_passed}, that its driver does not write:
it was added after the run. The report's README discloses these points.

\subsection{Stale and coordinate-dependent claims of the two reports}\label{fce:dx:sec:stale}
Nothing in either report was found to be wrong: every formula checked agrees
with Parts~I--V and with the data. Three statements are stale against this
report and are corrected here (5 October 2026).
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item Source~101-86 says that ``the particular conjectured order-three
recurrence is still unproved here'' (its README; its abstract and its
closing section say the proof does not use or prove it), and source~101-90 that ``no
particular guessed recurrence'' is asserted. True of the reports, but the
recurrence of Kauers and Koutschan is proved in Part~IV
(Theorem~\ref{fce:kk:thm:recurrence}), for every $n\ge0$.
\item Both report that a bounded source check ``found no proof of this exact
balanced-content equivalent'' (101-86) or ``no prior proof of this precise
full fixed-alphabet statement'' (101-90). As source-relative statements these
are not false; as priority statements they are superseded: the reports were
finished about nine hours before this report's first placement, and this
report holds four proofs of the same day (sources~61, 42, 75-03, 77-44); the
two reports' proofs are the fifth and the sixth.
\item Source~101-90's abstract ``accounts explicitly for the $r-1$ dominant
mark points''. Correct in its coordinates; the number is
coordinate-dependent (Remark~\ref{fce:dx:rem:invariant}): Section~6.4 has one
mark point.
\end{enumerate}
Source~101-90's ``the sequence is eventually strictly increasing'' is true and
is strengthened by Proposition~\ref{fce:dx:prop:supermult}.

\subsection{Further questions and research}\label{fce:dx:sec:questions}
The open questions of the two reports coincide with questions of
Section~\ref{fce:sec:future}, where they are now credited (dated notes of
October~5, 2026); none is answered by this Part. Each is listed with its
source, its sketch and what is missing.
\begin{enumerate}[label=101.\arabic*]
\item \emph{Explicit minimal recurrences} (101-90; its
D-finiteness is existence only, and 101-86 does not touch the recurrence).
Merged into ``Find and explain the fourth-row recurrence''. For $r=5$ an
order-three recurrence is proved (Part~IV); its minimality, and every other
row, remain open.
\item \emph{Smaller exponential sectors} beyond the Poincar\'e expansion
(101-86, 101-90). Merged into ``Determine the exponentially small sectors'';
for A215570 the candidate is the $(2/27)^n$ mode of
Remark~\ref{fce:kk:rem:scope}. Missing: connection constants and Stokes data.
\item \emph{Effective error constants for the inversion} (101-86: ``no
numerical error constant or unconditional exact-ceiling rule''; 101-90:
``effective numerical error bounds for inversion''). Merged into ``Determine
sharp asymptotic error constants''. Missing: explicit forms of the $O_J$ in
\eqref{fce:dx:eq:inverse} and of $\epsilon_J$, which would certify the single
ceiling outside an explicit window.
\item \emph{A growing alphabet} (101-90: ``uniform regimes in which the
alphabet size grows''). Merged into ``Let the alphabet grow''. Missing: root
separation and Gaussian bounds uniform in $r$; the constants
$\kappa_r$, $\det H_{\rm ex}=12(r-1)/(r^r(r+1))$ and the multiplicity $2h$
of Lemma~\ref{fce:dx:lem:torus} all move with $r$.
\item \emph{The field of the coefficients} (from 101-90's non-claim ``no
assertion that their smallest field is $\Q(\gamma_r)$ is needed''). Merged into
``Automate all-order coefficient extraction''. Every known value lies in
$\Q(\kappa_r)$: $\alpha_{5,j}\in\Q(\sqrt5)=\Q(\kappa_5)$
(Theorem~\ref{fce:thm:five}) and $\alpha_{4,1}\in\Q(\varrho)=\Q(\kappa_4)$
(Theorem~\ref{fce:ba:thm:alpha41}). Is $\alpha_{r,j}\in\Q(\kappa_r)$ for every
$r$ and $j$? The proofs only give the field generated by the coefficients of
the inside root factor, which may be larger for $r\ge6$.
\item \emph{An engine for every order} (101-86 shows that the Gaussian rule
reaches $j=2$ for $r=5$ in exact arithmetic; 101-90's rule is general).
Merged into ``Automate all-order coefficient extraction''. Missing: the
values $\alpha_{4,2}$, $\alpha_{4,3}$ and the rows $r=6,7,8$, which no
package computes.
\end{enumerate}
Two steps that the reports only sketch are \emph{not} open, because the
theorems they serve are proved in full elsewhere in this report: source~101-86's
common transfer domain (``a fixed outer radius between $1/4$ and $4/9$, with a
slit'') and source~101-90's ``any other branch collision on the critical
modulus is either one of the enumerated time copies or removable for the
selected product'' (Section~6.4 and Section~\ref{fce:subsec:allorders}, and the
operator-norm bound of Lemma~\ref{fce:ba:lem:global}).

\appendix
\section{A reproducible all-order construction}\label{fce:app:algorithm}
For clarity, the finite procedure implicit in the proof can be organized
as follows. The input is a fixed $r$ and a requested relative order $M$.

\paragraph{Critical algebraic data.}
Construct $Q_r$, remove $(u-1)^2$, and isolate the strict inside cluster.
Represent its symmetric functions in an algebraic number field. Solve
\eqref{fce:eq:criticalmoving} recursively for a finite Taylor jet of
$\tau(\boldsymbol y)$; obtain $\rho$, $\Phi$, and the tilted probabilities.
Internal root collisions can be handled by the coefficients of the
cluster factor instead of individual root labels.

\paragraph{Puiseux data.}
Set $t=\rho(\boldsymbol y)(1-w^2)$. Substitute
$u_0=\tau(\boldsymbol y)(1+\sum_{j\ge1}a_j(\boldsymbol y)w^j)$ in the
kernel, choosing $a_1=-\sqrt{2/V(\boldsymbol y)}$. The remaining
coefficients are obtained recursively. Multiply by the analytic
noncritical cluster product and the factor $1/t$ to form the needed
Puiseux jet of $\E$ and, independently, of $\log\E$.

\paragraph{Length coefficients.}
For every odd power $w^{2j+1}$, use \eqref{fce:eq:transfer} and the gamma-ratio
expansion. The even powers belong to the local analytic part. Retain
enough terms for the requested order, and include the length-period
factor $\nu$.

\paragraph{Composition coefficients.}
Expand the phase about zero, substitute
$\boldsymbol y=i\boldsymbol x/\sqrt N$, and multiply the amplitude jets.
Replace each even monomial by its centered Gaussian moment with covariance
$H^{-1}$; discard odd monomials. Wick's rule evaluates these moments as
finite sums over pairings. The determinant and common $\pi$ factor are
kept outside the normalized series.

\paragraph{Calibration and inversion.}
Divide the resulting excursion expansion by the logarithmic reference
expansion if desired. The exact coefficient \eqref{fce:eq:exactreference}
and Stirling's series are an independent normalization check. Convert
$N=rn$, compute the formal logarithm of the unit series, and use
\eqref{fce:eq:inversecorrectioneq} for inverse coefficients.

Every stage is finite at fixed $r,M$. The procedure proves computability,
not an optimal complexity bound or a claim that a complete general-purpose
implementation is included in this package. The supplied exact symbolic
implementation covers the first correction for $r=5$. Source~42's
formulation of the same procedure in lattice coordinates is
\eqref{fce:lg:eq:Gj}--\eqref{fce:lg:eq:betas}, its first-order
specialization is \eqref{fce:lg:eq:d1}, and its program covers the first
correction for $r=3$ and $r=5$. For $r=5$, source~75-03's program
(Part~\ref{fce:kk:part}) replaces the saddle construction above the first
order by the recurrence algorithm
\eqref{fce:kk:eq:ckalgorithm}--\eqref{fce:kk:eq:dalgorithm} and covers every
order; it has recorded $\alpha_{5,j}$ for $j\le8$. (2026-10-05 note: source~101-90 states the procedure in marker coordinates with the extreme
steps unmarked, \eqref{fce:dx:eq:recipe}, and its program covers $r=2,3$ to
second order; source~101-86's program carries it out for $r=5$ to second
order in exact arithmetic, without the recurrence,
Theorem~\ref{fce:dx:thm:alpha52}.)

\section{Package contents and build instructions}\label{fce:app:package}
The report ships the programs and recorded outputs of both sources under
prefixed names. Source~61 contributes \path{code/61-balanced-verify.py} and
\path{code/61-balanced-derive_alpha5.py}, their outputs
\path{data/61-balanced-*}, its source and claim provenance notes
\path{61-balanced-SOURCES.md} and its build receipt
\path{61-balanced-BUILD.md}. Source~42 contributes
\path{code/42-late-growing-verify.py} and
\path{code/42-late-growing-first_correction.py} and their outputs
\path{data/42-late-growing-*}. The programs read and write their outputs
under the delivered, unprefixed names in a \path{data} directory beside
\path{code}, and both verification programs write a file named
\path{verification.txt}; they must therefore be run on separate copies in
the delivered layout. The report's README gives the commands.

Source~75-03 (Part~\ref{fce:kk:part}, added October~2, 2026) contributes
\path{code/75-03-ballot-words-verify.py} and its certificate arrays
\path{code/75-03-ballot-words-certificate_data.py}, the recorded standard
output \path{data/75-03-ballot-words-verification_log.txt}, the exact
results \path{data/75-03-ballot-words-verification_results.json}, and
$\Lambda_n$ and $A_5(n)$ for $n\le103$ in
\path{data/75-03-ballot-words-a215570_terms.csv}. Its program imports
\path{certificate_data} by that delivered name and writes its JSON and CSV
beside itself under the delivered names; it must likewise be run on a copy
under those names, never in this directory.

Source~77-44 (Part~\ref{fce:ba:part}, added October~2, 2026) contributes
the driver \path{code/77-44-bal-verify.py}, the exact checker
\path{code/77-44-bal-check.py}, the derivation scripts
\path{code/77-44-bal-ballot_first_correction.py},
\path{code/77-44-bal-ballot_four_correction.py},
\path{code/77-44-bal-verify_ballot_kernels.py},
\path{code/77-44-bal-verify_ballot_large_dp.py} and
\path{code/77-44-bal-verify_ballot_numerical_derivatives.py}, and the
receipts \path{data/77-44-bal-*}. Its driver checks the hash of its
unshipped manuscript, so it runs only in the delivered layout, rebuilt from
the arrival archive; three of its scripts write their JSON receipts beside
themselves. The README gives the commands.

Sources~101-90 and~101-86 (Part~\ref{fce:dx:part}, added October~5, 2026)
contribute, under the prefixes \texttt{101-90-alphabet-} and
\texttt{101-86-five-}, their drivers \path{code/101-90-alphabet-run_checks.py}
and \path{code/101-86-five-run_checks.py}, their check programs in
\path{code/} (source~101-86's review program as
\path{code/101-86-five-audit-check_second_assembly_fast.py}), their recorded
outputs, receipts and QA files in \path{data/}, and their review notes
\path{101-90-alphabet-structural_review.md} and
\path{101-86-five-structural_review.md} (Section~\ref{fce:dx:sec:checks}).
The programs expect the delivered layout: each check program writes its JSON
output beside itself in a directory \path{checks}, each driver writes
\path{check_receipt.json} one level up, and source~101-86's review program,
delivered in \path{checks/audit}, reads \path{../second_correction.json}. They
must be run on a copy rebuilt in that layout, never in this directory; the
README gives the commands.

The computations use arbitrary-precision integers, rational arithmetic,
SymPy and mpmath (the shipped \path{data/61-balanced-requirements.txt},
whose content is identical to source~42's requirements file); they need no
network access. Source~42's \texttt{--quick} switch reduces its
dynamic-programming boxes; it changes the recorded finite ranges, not the
mathematical results, and it overwrites the diagnostic data with the
smaller run. Source URLs and the origin of the two externally supplied
large-index values are recorded in \path{61-balanced-SOURCES.md}. No
external paper is redistributed. The report is built with pdf\LaTeX; its
bibliography is embedded, so no BibTeX run is needed.

\section{The full rational telescoping certificate (source~75-03)}\label{fce:kk:app:certificate}
This appendix belongs to Part~IV and gives every coefficient needed in
\eqref{fce:kk:eq:cert1}--\eqref{fce:kk:eq:cert2}. Put $m=n+2$ (a local
letter, not a length), and
\[
 \mathfrak R_\sharp(m)=65m^3+203m^2+180m+48,\qquad
 \mathfrak S_\sharp(m)=65m^3+8m^2-31m+6,
\]
which are $\mathfrak R(n)$ and $\mathfrak S(n)$ of
Section~\ref{fce:kk:sec:recurrence} after the shift. Let
\begin{equation}\label{fce:kk:eq:certificateUV}
 \mathscr U_n(t)=-\frac{2t}{m(t^2-1)^2}\sum_{j=0}^8\mathfrak u_j(m)t^j,
 \qquad
 \mathscr V_n(t)=\frac{2t^2}{m(t^2-1)^2}\sum_{j=0}^8\mathfrak v_j(m)t^j.
\end{equation}
The coefficient polynomials $\mathfrak u_j,\mathfrak v_j$ (source~75-03's
$\alpha_j,\beta_j$, not the asymptotic coefficients) are printed below,
split into powers of $t$ so that no numerator term is omitted. The only
parameter denominator is $m=n+2$, harmless for $n\ge0$.

\subsection{Coefficients of \texorpdfstring{$\mathscr U_n$}{U-n}}
\begin{align*}
\mathfrak u_0={}&9m(m-1)(3m-2)(3m-1)(3m+1)\mathfrak R_\sharp(m),\\
\mathfrak u_1={}&3m(3m-2)(3m-1)(3m+1)\mathfrak R_\sharp(m),\\
\mathfrak u_2={}&-3m(3m-1)(3m+1)(28m^2-47m+18)\mathfrak R_\sharp(m),\\
\mathfrak u_3={}&-6m(3m+1)(15m^2-18m+5)\mathfrak R_\sharp(m),\\
\mathfrak u_4={}&(3m+1)\bigl(13195m^7+14169m^6-32535m^5-19461m^4\\
 &\hspace{31mm}+15480m^3+5472m^2-1888m-384\bigr),\\
\mathfrak u_5={}&11765m^7+28943m^6+2891m^5-27207m^4\\
 &\hspace{10mm}-12320m^3+5368m^2+2848m-384,\\
\mathfrak u_6={}&-10920m^8-23899m^7+10511m^6+42923m^5\\
 &\hspace{10mm}+12369m^4-16208m^3-5624m^2+2368m+384,\\
\mathfrak u_7={}&-16(m-1)(m+1)(m+2)^2\mathfrak S_\sharp(m),\\
\mathfrak u_8={}&32m(m-1)(m+1)(m+2)^2\mathfrak S_\sharp(m).
\end{align*}

\subsection{Coefficients of \texorpdfstring{$\mathscr V_n$}{V-n}}
\begin{align*}
\mathfrak v_0={}&-3m(m-1)(3m-2)(3m-1)(3m+1)\mathfrak R_\sharp(m),\\
\mathfrak v_1={}&-6m(m-1)(3m-1)(3m+1)\mathfrak R_\sharp(m),\\
\mathfrak v_2={}&-6m(3m+1)\bigl(1300m^6+3475m^5+1269m^4\\
 &\hspace{31mm}-1895m^3-841m^2+244m+48\bigr),\\
\mathfrak v_3={}&-2m(3m+1)\bigl(715m^5+2103m^4+1265m^3\\
 &\hspace{31mm}-523m^2-168m+208\bigr),\\
\mathfrak v_4={}&43745m^8+126154m^7+67228m^6-57642m^5\\
 &\hspace{10mm}-46597m^4+5216m^3+6760m^2-480m-384,\\
\mathfrak v_5={}&24(m+1)^2\bigl(325m^5+495m^4-330m^3\\
 &\hspace{31mm}-202m^2+148m-16\bigr),\\
\mathfrak v_6={}&-16(m+1)\bigl(1430m^7+3556m^6+471m^5\\
 &\hspace{31mm}-2714m^4-307m^3+684m^2-72m-24\bigr),\\
\mathfrak v_7={}&-16(m+1)(m+2)^2(2m-1)\mathfrak S_\sharp(m),\\
\mathfrak v_8={}&32m(m+1)(m+2)^2(2m-1)\mathfrak S_\sharp(m).
\end{align*}

\subsection{How to verify the certificate without a guesser}
Form $\mathscr R_0,\mathscr R_1$ from the vectors $\boldsymbol\zeta_j$ of
Section~\ref{fce:kk:sec:recurrence}, insert \eqref{fce:kk:eq:certificateUV}
into the right sides of \eqref{fce:kk:eq:cert1}--\eqref{fce:kk:eq:cert2},
subtract the left sides and multiply by common denominators in
$\Q[n,t]$: every resulting polynomial coefficient is zero. The calculation
involves no root choices, no limits and no sequence values. The shipped
\path{code/75-03-ballot-words-certificate_data.py} stores the same
$\mathfrak u_j,\mathfrak v_j$ as integer coefficient arrays (in descending
powers of $m$), and \path{code/75-03-ballot-words-verify.py} reduces the two
residuals exactly to zero; the printed polynomials were expanded and
compared with those arrays at the intake (2026-10-02), with no difference.
The recurrence then needs only the residue representation
\eqref{fce:kk:eq:residue} and the vanishing residue of a rational
derivative.
